there’s one common denominator among Korean skin-care products, be they cleansers or toners, it’s that they prioritize the skin barrier. With this context, the best Korean exfoliators may seem a little counterintuitive—after all, face exfoliants can sometimes do more harm than good, relying on strong active ingredients (or high concentrations) and inadvertently leaving skin dry or irritated.\nVogue’s Favorite Korean Exfoliants\n\nBest Chemical Exfoliant: Innisfree Green Tea Enzyme 7% PHA Gentle Exfoliating Toner, $24\nBest Physical Exfoliant: Dr.G Brightening Peeling Gel, $18\nBest Peel Pads: Medicube Zero Pore Pads, $19\nBest for Oily Skin: Some by Mi AHA-BHA-PHA 30Days Miracle Toner, $22\nBest for Dry Skin: Mixsoon Bean Essence, $35\nBest for Mature Skin: Beauty of Joseon Ground Rice and Honey Glow Mask, $18\nBest for Acne-Prone Skin: Cosrx AHA/BHA Clarifying Treatment Toner, $15\nBest for Sensitive Skin: Anua Rice Enzyme Brightening Cleansing Powder, $20\n\nBut that’s the beauty of the Korean approach to resurfacing skin. “K-beauty favors gentle, more frequent exfoliation that protects the skin barrier,” says Jane Yoo, MD, a board-certified dermatologist in New York, NY. “To this effect, K-beauty utilizes low-concentration acids, enzyme powders, or peeling gels that roll dead skin off the surface.” These light-handed exfoliating formulas also considered a critical part of a daily routine aimed towards getting (and maintaining) glass skin—and a natural counterpart to moisturizing. “Exfoliating is usually followed by layers of hydration to support the barrier as the goal is the same: achieve smooth skin but with less irritation,” says Dr. Yoo. With that in mind, consider these worth adding to your regimen.\n\nBest Chemical Exfoliant: Innisfree Green Tea Enzyme 7% PHA Gentle Exfoliating Toner\n\nWhy We Love It: Among gentle exfoliants, it’s hard to beat Innisfree’s Green Tea Derived Enzyme Toner, which eschews the usual alpha and beta hydroxy acids for polyhydroxy acid, or PHA. PHA has a larger molecular size, which encourages it to exfoliate dead skin cells more slowly. In this formula, it’s paired with green tea and hyaluronic acid to “to hydrate, exfoliate, and soothe the skin,” says Dr. Y. Claire Chang, M.D., a board-certified dermatologist at UnionDerm in New York, NY, who says that for this reason, it’s ideal for all, including those with dry or sensitive skin. For that reason, we consider it among the best face exfoliants, full stop—Korean or otherwise.\nEditor’s Experience: “I can always count on this Innisfree toner to get my skin baby soft and smooth after just one use. I love that I can use it without the fear of irritation. Pairing the PHA and enzyms with hyaluronic acid and green tea was smart on their part, to keep the formula balanced.” —Kiana Murden, senior beauty shopping writer\n\n\n\n\n\nLove It\nLeave It\n\n\n\n\nSuitable for even very sensitive skin\nExfoliating effects tend to be subtle\n\n\nActively hydrates skin\n\n\n\n\n\nBest Toner Pads: Medicube Zero Pore Pads\n\nWhy We Love It: Medicube Zero Pore Pads has a lot going for it: Namely, Dr. Chang likes that the exfoliating pads are both easy to use and gentle on skin, thanks to the combination of “low-dose AHA and BHA to refine pores and smooth texture,” she says. The alpha hydroxy acids are courtesy of both lactic acid (arguably the gentlest AHA out there) and fruit extracts, while white willow bark serves as a natural source of BHA. They’re also dual-sided, so you can use the textured side for additional physical exfoliation or a soft, smooth side for hydration.\nEditor’s Experience: I haven’t used these in a few months, and I can certainly tell in my daily complexion. I find the Zero Pore Pads particularly effective for morning use, ahead of my vitamin C; that way, I’m not relying on just nighttime for exfoliation. And because they’re gentle, I don’t have to worry about irritation throughout the day. —Kiana Murden, beauty shopping editor\n\n\n\n\n\nLove It\nLeave It\n\n\n\n\nCan improve the appearance of pores\nThe remaining serum has a slightly sticky feel\n\n\nSkin looks and feels smoother quickly\n\n\n\n\n\nBest for Mature Skin: Beauty of Joseon Ground Rice and Honey Glow Mask\n\nWhy We Love It: This wash-off mask gives you the best of both worlds, pairing gentle exfoliation from rice hull powder with a nourishing base of honey. Together, they improve uneven skin texture and tone, leaving skin smoother and more radiant. Another, more unexpected benefit: The mask has a clay base that helps clear pores, making it helpful for those dealing with perimenopause breakouts, but that honey (and honey extract) help offset any dryness.\n\n\n\n\n\nLove It\nLeave It\n\n\n\n\nLeaves skin glowing\nThe scent is off-putting to some\n\n\nEasy to remove\n\n\n\n\n\nBest for Oily Skin: Some by Mi AHA-BHA-PHA 30Days Miracle Toner\n\nWhy We Love It: Some By Mi’s aptly named Miracle Toner is about as powerful and acid-packed as it gets among Korean exfoliants. It pairs alpha, beta, and polyhydroxy acids to refine skin and slough away dead skin cells, “as well as niacinamide to boost skin cell turnover, brighten the skin, and reduce hyperpigmentation,” says Dr. Chang, who’s a fan. The niacinamide and tea tree extract also serve to control excess sebum production, leaving skin clean and balanced but not overly dry.\n\n\n\n\n\nLove It\nLeave It\n\n\n\n\nHelps with both breakouts and acne scars\nContains peppermint oil, which may be irritating to some\n\n\nHas a minty scent\n\n\n\n\n\nBest for Dry Skin: Mixsoon Bean Essence\n\nWhy We Love It: Essences are best known for delivering instant hydration, but Mixsoon’s formula bridges the gap with its eponymous bean essence—technically, soybean ferment extract. The ingredient has innate resurfacing properties, allowing the formula to remove dead skin cells and smooth uneven skin texture. All of the ingredients—including barley, pomegranate, and pear—have been fermented, which breaks molecules into smaller compounds so they better penetrate skin.\nEditor’s Experience: “If you know me, you’re familiar with my love of an essence. Of the one’s I keep in my vanity, Mixsoon’s is a frontrunner. I quite like the sticky texture that first sits on skin’s surface and sinks in after a few seconds to lock in moisture. The sensorial aspect is what initially drew me to it, though the results kept me reaching for it. Who knew a cocktail of ferments could diminish enlarged pores overnight?” —K.M.\n\n\n\n\n\nLove It\nLeave It\n\n\n\n\nGive skin a dewy finish\nMay be a little sensitizing on reactive skin types\n\n\nHelps clarify and decongest skin in just a few uses\nHas a slightly tacky feel some may not like\n\n\n\n\nBest for Dullness: Neogen Bio-Peel Gauze Peeling Green Tea\n\nWhy We Love It: Charlotte Cho, an esthetician and founder of Soko Glam, has raved about these peel pads for years. “They’re one of the most iconic exfoliating products in K-beauty for a reason,” she says. “I love how the dual-textured pads give you the satisfaction of a physical exfoliant, but are soaked in a gentle essence that keeps the skin smooth and glowy without over-stripping.” Green tea and Centella asiatica (you might recognize it as cica) soothe redness and offset discoloration, leaving skin more radiant over time.\n\n\n\n\n\nLove It\nLeave It\n\n\n\n\nDouble-sided pads offer extra versatility\n30 pads won’t last long\n\n\nDoesn’t irritate or dry out skin\n\n\n\n\n\nBest for Sensitive Skin: Anua Rice Enzyme Brightening Cleansing Powder\n\nWhy We Love It: Just add water to transform this rice powder-based formula into a sudsy foam. Once lathered, the formula deploys papin, an enzyme that’s derived from papaya and helps dissolve the glue that binds dead skin cells. But it has other advantages, too, with “alpha-arbutin for brightening and a lot of calming ingredients, such as ceramides, allantoin, and glycerin, to help support the skin,” says Dr. Yoo, who likes that it’s especially gentle. It’s also versatile: You can also mix a larger amount of the powder with water (or even toner) to create a bouncy formula that doubles as a rinse-off mask.\nEditor’s Experience: Testing the product for the time, I dumped a little more than a nickel-sized amount of powder into my hand, then created the paste-like goop by mixing with water. Once tacky, I massaged onto my face like usual. Honestly, the texture of it was fun to play with—it made my nighttime routine feel like a teenage science experiment, rather than a monotonous daily chore. As pleased as I was with the actual practice of cleansing, I was just as satisfied with the results—clean, soft, and supple are the words I jotted down after my initial trial.” —K.M.\n\n\n\n\n\nLove It\nLeave It\n\n\n\n\nHas powerful brightening ingredients\nCan take some effort to rinse off\n\n\nTravel-friendly formulation\nRequires massaging into skin for at least 60 seconds\n\n\n\n\nBest Physical Exfoliant: Dr. G Brightening Peeling Gel\n\nWhy We Love It: Forget scrubs—this gel relies on a type of exfoliation called gommage, which “you massage into dry or damp skin until it clumps up into flakes that are then rinsed off,” says Dr. Yoo. The Dr. G Peeling Gel accomplishes this via cellulose, “which is a polymer that allows exfoliation through friction and meshes with surface debris and impurities on the skin and forms fragments,” she explains, adding that it’s still much gentler than physical scrubs used stateside. Other advantages: It has a vitamin C derivative to help even out skin tone, while allantoin and ceramides help sooth and support the skin barrier.\n\n\n\n\n\nLove It\nLeave It\n\n\n\n\nGentle enough for sensitive skin\nNothing to note\n\n\nEasy to use\n\n\n\n\n\nBest for Acne-Prone Skin: CosRx AHA/BHA Clarifying Treatment Toner\n\nWhy We Love It: For those with congested skin, the CosRx AHA/BHA Clarifying Treatment Toner is potent enough to keep pores clear without overly stripping skin. With this context, the best Korean exfoliators may seem a little counterintuitive—after all, face exfoliants can sometimes do more harm than good, relying on strong active ingredients (or high concentrations) and inadvertently leaving skin dry or irritated.Vogue’s Favorite Korean ExfoliantsBest Chemical Exfoliant: Innisfree Green Tea Enzyme 7% PHA Gentle Exfoliating Toner, $24
Best Physical Exfoliant: Dr.G Brightening Peeling Gel, $18
Best Peel Pads: Medicube Zero Pore Pads, $19
Best for Oily Skin: Some by Mi AHA-BHA-PHA 30Days Miracle Toner, $22
Best for Dry Skin: Mixsoon Bean Essence, $35
Best for Mature Skin: Beauty of Joseon Ground Rice and Honey Glow Mask, $18
Best for Acne-Prone Skin: Cosrx AHA/BHA Clarifying Treatment Toner, $15
Best for Sensitive Skin: Anua Rice Enzyme Brightening Cleansing Powder, $20
But that’s the beauty of the Korean approach to resurfacing skin. “K-beauty favors gentle, more frequent exfoliation that protects the skin barrier,” says Jane Yoo, MD, a board-certified dermatologist in New York, NY. “To this effect, K-beauty utilizes low-concentration acids, enzyme powders, or peeling gels that roll dead skin off the surface.” These light-handed exfoliating formulas also considered a critical part of a daily routine aimed towards getting (and maintaining) glass skin—and a natural counterpart to moisturizing. “Exfoliating is usually followed by layers of hydration to support the barrier as the goal is the same: achieve smooth skin but with less irritation,” says Dr. Yoo. With that in mind, consider these worth adding to your regimen.
In This Story:
What are the benefits of exfoliating?What do Koreans exfoliate with?Should you exfoliate daily?How We TestedMeet the ExpertsBest Chemical Exfoliant: Innisfree Green Tea Enzyme 7% PHA Gentle Exfoliating TonerSave to wishlistSave to wishlistInnisfree
## Green Tea Enzyme 7% PHA Gentle Exfoliating Toner
AMAZON
Why We Love It: Among gentle exfoliants, it’s hard to beat Innisfree’s Green Tea Derived Enzyme Toner, which eschews the usual alpha and beta hydroxy acids for polyhydroxy acid, or PHA. PHA has a larger molecular size, which encourages it to exfoliate dead skin cells more slowly. In this formula, it’s paired with green tea and hyaluronic acid to “to hydrate, exfoliate, and soothe the skin,” says Dr. Y. Claire Chang, M.D., a board-certified dermatologist at UnionDerm in New York, NY, who says that for this reason, it’s ideal for all, including those with dry or sensitive skin. For that reason, we consider it among the best face exfoliants, full stop—Korean or otherwise.
Editor’s Experience: “I can always count on this Innisfree toner to get my skin baby soft and smooth after just one use. I love that I can use it without the fear of irritation. Pairing the PHA and enzyms with hyaluronic acid and green tea was smart on their part, to keep the formula balanced.” —Kiana Murden, senior beauty shopping writer
More to Know
Key Ingredients: Polyhydroxy acid, green tea, hyaluronic acidType: ChemicalSize: 150 mlLove ItLeave ItSuitable for even very sensitive skinExfoliating effects tend to be subtleActively hydrates skinBest Toner Pads: Medicube Zero Pore PadsSave to wishlistSave to wishlistMedicube
## Zero Pore Pads
AMAZON
Why We Love It: Medicube Zero Pore Pads has a lot going for it: Namely, Dr. Chang likes that the exfoliating pads are both easy to use and gentle on skin, thanks to the combination of “low-dose AHA and BHA to refine pores and smooth texture,” she says. The alpha hydroxy acids are courtesy of both lactic acid (arguably the gentlest AHA out there) and fruit extracts, while white willow bark serves as a natural source of BHA. They’re also dual-sided, so you can use the textured side for additional physical exfoliation or a soft, smooth side for hydration.
Editor’s Experience: I haven’t used these in a few months, and I can certainly tell in my daily complexion. I find the Zero Pore Pads particularly effective for morning use, ahead of my vitamin C; that way, I’m not relying on just nighttime for exfoliation. And because they’re gentle, I don’t have to worry about irritation throughout the day. —Kiana Murden, beauty shopping editor
More to Know
Key Ingredients: Lactic acid, white willow bark extract, cypress tree leaf extractType: Chemical and physicalSize: 70 or 100 padsLove ItLeave ItCan improve the appearance of poresThe remaining serum has a slightly sticky feelSkin looks and feels smoother quicklyBest for Mature Skin: Beauty of Joseon Ground Rice and Honey Glow MaskSave to wishlistSave to wishlistBeauty of Joseon
## Ground Rice and Honey Glow Mask
SHOP
Why We Love It: This wash-off mask gives you the best of both worlds, pairing gentle exfoliation from rice hull powder with a nourishing base of honey. Together, they improve uneven skin texture and tone, leaving skin smoother and more radiant. Another, more unexpected benefit: The mask has a clay base that helps clear pores, making it helpful for those dealing with perimenopause breakouts, but that honey (and honey extract) help offset any dryness.
More to Know
Key Ingredients: Kaolin, honey, glycerin, rice powder, rice bran, rice extractType: PhysicalSize: 5.07 fl. oz.Love ItLeave ItLeaves skin glowingThe scent is off-putting to someEasy to removeBest for Oily Skin: Some by Mi AHA-BHA-PHA 30Days Miracle TonerSave to wishlistSave to wishlistSome By Mi
## AHA BHA PHA 30 Days Miracle Toner
SOKO GLAM
Why We Love It: Some By Mi’s aptly named Miracle Toner is about as powerful and acid-packed as it gets among Korean exfoliants. It pairs alpha, beta, and polyhydroxy acids to refine skin and slough away dead skin cells, “as well as niacinamide to boost skin cell turnover, brighten the skin, and reduce hyperpigmentation,” says Dr. Chang, who’s a fan. The niacinamide and tea tree extract also serve to control excess sebum production, leaving skin clean and balanced but not overly dry.
More to Know
Key Ingredients: Salicylic acid, niacinamide, tea tree extractType: ChemicalSize: 150 mlLove ItLeave ItHelps with both breakouts and acne scarsContains peppermint oil, which may be irritating to someHas a minty scentBest for Dry Skin: Mixsoon Bean EssenceSave to wishlistSave to wishlistMixsoon
## Bean Essence
SOKO GLAM
Why We Love It: Essences are best known for delivering instant hydration, but Mixsoon’s formula bridges the gap with its eponymous bean essence—technically, soybean ferment extract. The ingredient has innate resurfacing properties, allowing the formula to remove dead skin cells and smooth uneven skin texture. All of the ingredients—including barley, pomegranate, and pear—have been fermented, which breaks molecules into smaller compounds so they better penetrate skin.
Editor’s Experience: “If you know me, you’re familiar with my love of an essence. Of the one’s I keep in my vanity, Mixsoon’s is a frontrunner. I quite like the sticky texture that first sits on skin’s surface and sinks in after a few seconds to lock in moisture. The sensorial aspect is what initially drew me to it, though the results kept me reaching for it. Who knew a cocktail of ferments could diminish enlarged pores overnight?” —K.M.
More to Know
Key Ingredients: Soybean ferment, barley ferment, pomegranate ferment, pear juice fermentType: ChemicalSize: 50 mlLove ItLeave ItGive skin a dewy finishMay be a little sensitizing on reactive skin typesHelps clarify and decongest skin in just a few usesHas a slightly tacky feel some may not likeBest for Dullness: Neogen Bio-Peel Gauze Peeling Green TeaSave to wishlistSave to wishlistNeogen
## Bio-Peel Gauze Peeling Pads
AMAZON
SOKO GLAM
Why We Love It: Charlotte Cho, an esthetician and founder of Soko Glam, has raved about these peel pads for years. “They’re one of the most iconic exfoliating products in K-beauty for a reason,” she says. “I love how the dual-textured pads give you the satisfaction of a physical exfoliant, but are soaked in a gentle essence that keeps the skin smooth and glowy without over-stripping.” Green tea and Centella asiatica (you might recognize it as cica) soothe redness and offset discoloration, leaving skin more radiant over time.
More to Know
Key Ingredients: Green tea extract, Centella asiatica, tea tree extract, tea tree oilType: Chemical and physicalSize: 30 padsLove ItLeave ItDouble-sided pads offer extra versatility30 pads won’t last longDoesn’t irritate or dry out skinBest for Sensitive Skin: Anua Rice Enzyme Brightening Cleansing PowderSave to wishlistSave to wishlistAnua
## Rice Enzyme Brightening Powder
AMAZON
Why We Love It: Just add water to transform this rice powder-based formula into a sudsy foam. Once lathered, the formula deploys papin, an enzyme that’s derived from papaya and helps dissolve the glue that binds dead skin cells. But it has other advantages, too, with “alpha-arbutin for brightening and a lot of calming ingredients, such as ceramides, allantoin, and glycerin, to help support the skin,” says Dr. Yoo, who likes that it’s especially gentle. It’s also versatile: You can also mix a larger amount of the powder with water (or even toner) to create a bouncy formula that doubles as a rinse-off mask.
Editor’s Experience: Testing the product for the time, I dumped a little more than a nickel-sized amount of powder into my hand, then created the paste-like goop by mixing with water. Once tacky, I massaged onto my face like usual. Honestly, the texture of it was fun to play with—it made my nighttime routine feel like a teenage science experiment, rather than a monotonous daily chore. As pleased as I was with the actual practice of cleansing, I was just as satisfied with the results—clean, soft, and supple are the words I jotted down after my initial trial.” —K.M.
More to Know
Key Ingredients: Papain, rice powder, rice bran water, ceramides, ascorbic acid, alpha-arbutinType: ChemicalSize: 1.41 oz.Love ItLeave ItHas powerful brightening ingredientsCan take some effort to rinse offTravel-friendly formulationRequires massaging into skin for at least 60 secondsBest Physical Exfoliant: Dr. G Brightening Peeling GelSave to wishlistSave to wishlistDr.G
## Brightening Peeling Gel
AMAZON
Why We Love It: Forget scrubs—this gel relies on a type of exfoliation called gommage, which “you massage into dry or damp skin until it clumps up into flakes that are then rinsed off,” says Dr. Yoo. The Dr. G Peeling Gel accomplishes this via cellulose, “which is a polymer that allows exfoliation through friction and meshes with surface debris and impurities on the skin and forms fragments,” she explains, adding that it’s still much gentler than physical scrubs used stateside. Other advantages: It has a vitamin C derivative to help even out skin tone, while allantoin and ceramides help sooth and support the skin barrier.
More to Know
Key Ingredients: Cellulose, allantoin, panthenol, glycerin, ascorbic acid, hyaluronic acidType: PhysicalSize: 4.23 fl. oz.Love ItLeave ItGentle enough for sensitive skinNothing to noteEasy to useBest for Acne-Prone Skin: CosRx AHA/BHA Clarifying Treatment TonerSave to wishlistSave to wishlist
## AHA/BHA Treatment Toner
AMAZON
Why We Love It: For those with congested skin, the CosRx AHA/BHA Clarifying Treatment Toner is potent enough to keep pores clear without overly stripping skin. Thanks to a combination of 0.1% glycolic acid (a comparatively low concentration) and betaine salicylate (a derivative of salicylic acid), the toner is able “to remove dead skin cells, unclog pores, and reduce blemishes,” says Dr. Chang. “After regular use, it can leave the skin glowing and radiant.” Despite its acid-heavy lineup, however, she’s found that even those with sensitive skin tend to tolerate it well—possibly due to the inclusion of soothing allantoin and panthenol.
More to Know
Key Ingredients: Glycolic acid, beta hydroxy acid, willow bark water, apple fruit water, allantoin, panthenolType: ChemicalSize: 150 mlLove ItLeave ItVery low concentrations of acids work for most skin typesCan take weeks and consistent use to see resultsDoesn’t have a noticeable scentEverything You Need to KnowWhat are the benefits of exfoliating?
Exfoliation works on multiple levels to refine and resurface skin—making it one of the most impactful steps in any skin-care routine. At its most basic, “exfoliation helps to remove the outermost layer of dead skin cells in the stratum corneum, promoting skin cell turnover and revealing brighter, smoother and more even-toned skin,” says Dr. Chang.
It can also clear debris from pores, which helps reduce whiteheads, blackheads, and other forms of acne. And in helping shed those dead skin cells that would otherwise pile up on skin’s surface, “exfoliation can also help improve the absorption of active ingredients like vitamin C or retinoids,” she says.
What do Koreans exfoliate with?
Unlike Western exfoliation habits—which tend to be more aggressive—“Korean skin-care routines emphasize the regular use of gentler topicals to achieve radiant skin without irritation,” says Dr. Chang. One big point of difference is that in the U.S., many formulations tend to focus on correcting issues, be they acne, dullness, or dark spots.
However, Koreans prioritize prevention and consistency. As a result, “you’ll rarely find harsh scrubs or aggressive peels,” says Cho. “The majority of K-beauty exfoliants use mild chemical exfoliants such as PHA, because they clear dead skin cells without irritation.” Fermented enzymes and natural fruit extracts are also popular, since they tend to be well tolerated even by those with sensitive skin, according to Dr. Chang.
Should you exfoliate daily?
How often you exfoliate ultimately depends on your skin type and the sort of exfoliant you’re using. For example, if you have dry or sensitive skin, exfoliating once or twice a week with a mild acid may suffice, says Dr. Chang. Meanwhile, “for those with oily or acne-prone skin, people may tolerate light daily exfoliation with ingredients like salicylic acid,” she says.
Ultimately, the strength of your exfoliant plays a large role. “Higher strength exfoliants should be used less frequently, while very gentle exfoliators could be used daily in those who tolerate it well,” says Dr. Chang. If you’re using a Korean exfoliant, then it’s likely gentle enough to use daily or every other day (but again, that depends). Ultimately, she says, “the key is to monitor for signs of over-exfoliation—like stinging, redness, flaking, or tightness—and adjust the frequency accordingly.”
How We Tested
When we test and review a product, we take a holistic approach to deliver well-rounded product recommendations. First, we lean on Vogue’s vast network of experts—from board-certified dermatologists to celebrity estheticians—to gain professional acumen on the industry’s standout products, ones these specialists would actually use on their clients. We pair their expertise with our editorial best practices to curate the thoughtful edits you read on our site.
As it relates to the best Korean exfoliator, we selected the best based on the following characteristics: ingredients, texture, exfoliant type, and the skin-care concerns they address. To do this, we paired our own personal tests of each formula with expert guidance and reviewer insights to determine which we would recommend to you.
